      In JULY 1952, President HARRY S TRUMAN and his Chairman of the Joint
Chiefs of Staff, General OMAR N. BRADLEY as the highest commanders of the
United States armed forces ,acted openly:

    ("DEFENSE DEPARTMENT ORDERS TO SHOOT DOWN UFO’S WHICH REFUSE TO LAND)
        WHEN ORDERED TO DO SO."

      The essential necessity of this ARMED DEFENSE against the UFO forces were
the series of mysterious disasters and accidents within the military forces,-
the UFO units agressive reconnaisance from May to July of 1952, over and around
the capital,~ Washington D.C., especially on July 26-27, when they appeared
above the White House, the Capitol and the security establishments of the nation’s
capital. It had been justified not in the last row the public increasing anxiety
generated by the media’s unabated UFO reports from allover the world.

        Doc.6 :         MARCH, 1954. U.s. OFFICE OF THE JOINT CHIEFS OF STAFF
      The  JA1~AP
                146 /1952 (*) is the FIRST supporting written document of U.S.A.
two highest responsible authorities above dispatch as effective action for the
national security.

      The JANAP 146(C) was the third revised and superseded Communication
Instruction for Reporting Vital Intelligence Sightings, abbreviated as CIRVIS.
The CIRVIS report priority-communication is provided for observed suspicious
airborne and ground activities,- the MERINT reporting procedure is for the same
in waterborne relations.

                      U.s. Navy Admiral James S. RUSSEL, Vice Chief of Naval Operation super-
                seded MERINT communication instruction in the year of 1959 justifies, that
                from 1952, the Joint Chiefs of Staff in unanimity with the Canadian defense
                forces had        ex~ded
                                    the territorial warning network to the whole Americau
                continent, and the Atlantic and Pacific waters.

                      In the Foreword Admiral Russel strictly emphasize that "TIMELY RECEIPT OF
                REPORTS OF SIGHTINGS WILL ENEBLE MILITARY FORCES TO TAKE PROMPT DEFENSIVE OR
                INVESTIGATIVE ACTION."

               1. This pamphlet is designed familiarize masters of  ~o
               merchant ships and fishing vessels and radio orricers with
               the procedures ror reporting vital intelligence sightings.
               The reports are desired ror early warning in derense or the
               North A~erican Continent, United States and Canadian Terri-
               tories and Possessions. Timely receipt or reports of sightings
               will enable military forces to take prompt defensive or
               investigative action.

    On a more positive note, you may find it useful to know that the Public Records
    Office at Kew, Richmond, Surrey retains governmental files which are over thirty
    years old and as such they may be able to help you further, although I am aware that
    during the ’sixties’ the MOD policy was to destroy UFO reports after five years on
    file, which may account for a dearth of reports for that period.

    I hope that you will find this helpful.

                                              .t
           .i)vertake. Jfthe segment was 6 o’clock, then we had about
                                                                     .
                                                                                      knots of overtake. The
            GaXimumovertake was in.the9 o’clock position. The overtake I had on this particular
             intercept was in the 7 or 8 o’clock position which indicate close to 100 knot overtake. Iwas
             really hauling coals. To complete the description of the radar scope there were two other
             significant pieces of data displayed. One is the horizontal indicator which gave a gyro


                                                                                                                      ~
             stabilized reference to the horizon enabling the pilot to not have to refer to his flight
             instruments. The second is a steering dot, which was nothing more than computer data
             indicating which way the aircraft should fly to accomplish the intercept. i.e. if the dot was
             above the center, the stick should be pulled back to climb, if it was to the right then turn to
             the right to center the dot. The idea was to have the dot centered in the smaller circle.
            A normal intercept proceeds from the lock on phase with the constant maneuvering to center
            the dot. When the aircraft is in a position to accomplish its intercept, the dot would be
            centered. The outer circle will start to shrink at 20 seconds from rocket release. The circle in
            the center shri~ks to about a Quarter inch, and keeping the dot centered requires small rapid
            maneuvers. At about the time the outer circle reaches three Quarters of an inch in diameter a
            small Quarter inch line appears in lieu of the inner circle. This is the signal to pull the trigger
            for rocket release, and to make only up and down corrections as the computer calculates the
            point of rocket release for the azimuth. With the trigger pulled and the switches set, the
            rockets are released by the computer.
            Now back to the intercept of Ute UFO. As I said I had an overtake of 800 knots and my radar
            was rock stable. The dot was centered and only the slightest corrections were necessary. This
            was a very fast intercept and the circle started to shrink. I called "20 secondsR and the GCI
            indicated he was standing by. The overtake was still indicating in the 7 or 8 o’clock position.
            At about 10 seconds to go, I noticed that the overtake position was changing its position. It
            moved rapidly to the 6 o’clock then 3 o’clock then 12 o’clock and finally rested about the 11
            o’clock position. This indicated a negative overtake of 200 knots (the maximum negative
            overtake displayed). There was no way of knowing of what the actual speed of the UFO was as
            he could be traveling at very high mach numbers and I would only lee the 200 knot negative
            overtake. The circle, which was down to about an inch and a half in diameter, .tarted to open
            up rapidly. Witl n leconds it was back to 3 inches in diameter, and the blip was visible in the
            blackened jizzle band moving up the acope. This meant that it was loing away from me. I

                                         UFO TYPES

In order to differentiate concisely but adequately between the many UFO shapes
and types reported, the following catalogue describes and (for future reference)
codes the various forms presently recognized by Data Research. These codes
appear in a few of the summaries and analyses, detailed on later pages. There
are a few new additions to the codes since our last publication of the UFO
Register as some of the witnesses descriptions could not be catagorised within
the existing list.

CODE
    1a    Flat disc:   round, domeless.
    1b      II     "   multisided, domeless.
    1c      "      "   round, centrally domed. Domes variable in size.
    1d      " " or Dustbin-lid       shaped: round, sub-pyramidal, usually domeless.
    1e      "    "     with Antennae
    2     Hatshaped:   central "cabin vertically cylindrical (but often rounded
                                                                              .     on
                       top)    encircled ventrally by  a flat rim or flange.
    3a    Bowl or Dish shaped: round, domeless.
    3b      "        "    "        round, domed, or sometimes with central "spindletl
                                   like device.
    4     Saucer shaped: round, centrally domed. Domes variously proportioned.
    5a    Double saucer shaped: round, slightly convex units, joined peripherally.
    5b        "      "      II     round, markedly convex units, similarly joined.
    r
    o     Rugby-ball shaped:       domeless.
    6a        II   "    "          centrally domed. Domes usually small or very small.
    6b        II   II   II         with small h:.teral fins.
    7     Saturn-like: encircling flange joined to main orb-shaped body of object.
    8a    Sphere or Globe: Plain surfaced. Sometimes with "ports" or apertures.
    8b
    8c

              " II "           surface panelled or segmented.
                               tailed. Tails of various shapes and lengths.
                                                                                           ~
    8d        " " "            domed. Domes usually very small; can be situated on
                               both the top and underside of main body of object.
    8e    Sphere:          winged
    8f      "              with "feet"
    9     Egg-shaped:
    10    Round:    a blanket description. Either types 1a to . 4 viewed full-face,
                    or an indistinct manifestation of type 8a.

          Elliptical or Oval: either that shape or oblique views of types 1a-5a.
          Oval:     has body transversely bisected by bar-like structure.
                    has elongated shape. Variant-possibly of type 42.
          Pyramidal: pitch to apex variable (possibly a variant of type 1d)
          Cone shaped: one end rounded. Often described as bullet-like or shell-
                        like (military, not molluscan shells are meant here).
    13b     "          "as type 13a but with one end pointed.
    14a   Rocket-like: single or multi-finned.
    14b     II    II        finless.

    16    Torpedo shaped: (including those forme described as "sausage"-like).

    18b     II           "      wholly or partially tapered.
    18c   Funnel-like or Bell shaped: may be an acute variant of type 18b.
    18d   Torch shaped:       cylindrical in main body but one end fanned-out.
    19    Rod-like:        usually very thin and sometimes very long (possibly a variant
                           of type 18a).

                      arm-like structures, often independently movable.

       Diamond or Lozenge shaped: nearly always domeless and usually flat.

                                        altering shape continuously.

52 Jeep-like (minus wheels): occasionally with a central turret.
53 Boot -shaped: may be connected with type 50 (see Stanway and Pace 1968.)
                      "Flying Saucer Report: UFOs Unidentified, Undeniable".P.11
54   House or Haystack  shaped: apparently a roofed-in box-like form.

56 Triglobular:
5611 Bi-globular: as far as known only small size objects.

                                                             with two or more
                             tails.

                           describable shape. Possible th se indeterminate
                            shapes are more a result of poor eyewitness reporting.

63       Bird shaped:   (not mothman): usually gigantic, solid body of a general

briefest glance at the object, did not appear to be very
impressed.
When asked what it was about his sighting that caused the
witness to believe that the object was unusual, he replied that
"the rear of the object displayed two large luminous greenish-
white concave-shaped "ports"". Also, he declared that the dark
body of the object was, in some way, "ribbed", and that,
despite its bulk and extremely rapid mode of departure, no
sound could be detected at all. Strangely, despite the fact
that it was still light during this high-summer evening, and
that there were other people about, there were no other known
witnesses or reports in the local media.
